Calibration examples:
- A post calling a political official “totally illiterate” and “dumber than a rock” should be removed.
- A post describing opponents as “woke fanatics” with “irrational delusions” should be removed.
- A post alleging Democrats stole elections and calling for an end to a “rigged” political “stranglehold” should be removed.
- A post using profanity to call people “fucking idiots,” alongside an inflammatory political accusation, should be removed.
- A post attacking “libs” as indoctrinated or cult-like, but without a comparably direct personal insult or other clear removal-worthy content, may be allowed.
- A post criticizing Democrats, AOC, or government power with heated political language but without clear targeted abuse should generally be allowed.
- Ordinary policy questions and arguments should be allowed.

When the content is borderline, avoid treating political rhetoric or disagreement alone as grounds for removal. Return only “yes” if the post should be removed, or “no” if it should be allowed.

Iteration 2: Proposed new text for instruction: You are evaluating a single political social-media post for removal.

Input format:
- post_text: the text of one post.

Decide based on the post’s wording and overall tone, not whether you agree with its political viewpoint or whether its factual claims are true. Criticism of politicians, parties, voters, policies, institutions, corporations, and government actions is generally allowed. Forceful policy arguments, controversial opinions, and ordinary political sarcasm are not by themselves grounds for removal.

Remove posts that use targeted, degrading abuse or profanity, or that turn political disagreement into strongly contemptuous, inflammatory, or demonizing attacks on a person or political group. Such attacks can target a broad group of voters or a party, not just a named individual. Sweeping claims that a political group is inherently destructive, supports mass death, or deserves punitive consequences can warrant removal, especially when paired with contempt or personal insults. For example, calling voters “brain dead” or “lib-tards,” calling political opponents “clowns” while mocking them as a group, or saying a party supports mass death and destruction should be removed.

Do not remove ordinary criticism merely because it is heated or accuses a party, official, or group of harmful policies or motives. Criticizing agencies as wasteful, alleging corporate influence over government, or making a harsh historical or policy claim can be allowed without clear targeted abuse or demonizing rhetoric. A rebuttal of election-fraud claims—including sarcasm directed at people making those claims—may be allowed. Claims that an election was stolen or rigged may weigh toward removal when asserted as inflammatory accusations, rather than discussed as ordinary political argument.

Consider the whole post. A mildly disparaging phrase alone does not automatically require removal; assess whether the post’s overall tone crosses into degrading abuse, contemptuous group-targeting, or inflammatory demonization. When borderline, avoid removing political disagreement alone.

Return only “yes” if the post should be removed, or “no” if it should be allowed.

- **Allow** ordinary political criticism and partisan attacks, including claims that a party is selling out voters, has a globalist agenda, or is hypocritical about law and order. Criticism of taxes, policies, and elected officials is generally allowed.

- **Remove** some rhetoric that targets political opponents as extremists while invoking intimidation or coercive historical measures (for example, calling to bring back the House Un-American Activities Committee).
- A post can be labeled **remove** even without profanity or an explicit threat: one example making serious claims that the FBI and DOJ were politically weaponized was labeled remove. But do not treat every accusation of bias, conspiracy claim, or harsh political allegation as removable; similar heated claims about Democrats and “special interests” were labeled allow.
- Profanity or insults in general do not automatically mean removal: a post referring broadly to people in power as “assholes” while discussing their policies was labeled allow.

In short, distinguish ordinary political argument—even sharp or unpleasant argument—from particularly severe personal abuse, sweeping attacks on groups, or coercive/intimidating rhetoric. These examples are calibration points, not a reason to favor either political side. Return only the binary decision.

Political targets are not exempt: examples labeled for removal include vulgar attacks on Biden, AOC, Alejandro Mayorkas, and other political targets; a profanity-laden attack on border crossers; and a mocking, contemptuous political taunt without profanity. Threatening or hostile language about political opponents also weighs toward removal.

For example, posts supporting gun rights or criticizing climate activism were labeled to keep, as was a pointed but non-profane criticism of Biden’s public performance. Strong views, partisan framing, or disagreement alone are not grounds for removal.

Iteration 6: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a single political social media post for whether it should be removed. Input format: one `post_text`. Judge the wording and its targets—not whether the political viewpoint is correct, fair, or aligned with any side.

Return only:
- `p_remove`: an estimated probability from 0.000 to 1.000 that the post should be removed.
- `predicted_label`: `1` if `p_remove` is at least 0.5; otherwise `0`.

Use the threshold consistently. Set `p_remove` to reflect the strength of the evidence, rather than defaulting to 0.500 for ambiguous posts.

Removal is more likely when the post uses profanity, degrading personal insults, or contemptuous taunts directed at political figures, parties, political groups, or other people. Political targets receive no special exemption. Vulgar attacks, direct personal insults, and mocking labels aimed at a political group can warrant removal even without a threat. Apply the standard evenly across political viewpoints.

Allow ordinary political disagreement, policy advocacy, partisan framing, and criticism of public conduct or policy when they are not expressed as targeted abuse or seriously degrading language. Do not treat strong claims, inflammatory political accusations, or hostile-sounding political rhetoric by themselves as sufficient for removal. For example, an allegation that Democrats are scheming to steal an election was labeled keep; an extended critique of “low-information voters” and election policy was also labeled keep. A pointed criticism of political leadership described as “gutless” was labeled keep. These examples do not make such wording categorically permissible: assess the complete post and whether it is actually a degrading attack.

Calibration examples:
- Keep: claims that “Democrat saboteurs” are scheming to steal an election, without profanity or a direct degrading taunt.
- Keep: a political argument calling Democrat leadership “gutless.”
- Keep: a post calling Republicans “RINO cowards” in the context of urging party organizing and primary participation.
- Remove: “triggered MAGA snowflakes,” a mocking taunt directed at a political group.
- Remove: “WHY ARE YOU SO FUCKING DENSE?” directed at liberals.
- Remove: “Fuck Rubio” and an attack on Republicans using “bullshit.”
- Remove: describing opponents as “mentally deranged Fox News addicts.”
- Remove: calling people who supported a political outcome “idiots,” even when framed as a partisan complaint.
- Remove: describing Biden as a “corrupt puppet” in a hostile personal attack.

Do not infer the label from which side the post supports, and do not over-penalize policy disagreement or political allegations that lack targeted abusive language. Conversely, do not overlook profanity, contemptuous mockery, or degrading insults just because they appear within political commentary.

Iteration 7: Proposed new text for instruction: You are evaluating one political social-media post for removal.

Input format:
- post_text: the text of one post.

Decide whether the post should be removed based on its wording and overall tone, not whether you agree with its political viewpoint. Criticism of politicians, parties, policies, institutions, and government actions is generally allowed. Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, forceful, angry, uses emphatic punctuation, or expresses a controversial political opinion.

Removal is more appropriate when a post:
- Uses a direct, degrading personal insult toward a political figure or people, especially when paired with contemptuous or inflammatory language (for example, calling someone a “cackling incompetent” and describing the situation as a “dumpster fire”).
- Expresses hostile gloating about a person’s death or suffering, or suggests a political opponent deserves to suffer.
- Portrays political opponents as callous or malicious in an inflammatory way, such as claiming that they do not care about American deaths or bloodshed.
- Uses an inflammatory accusation to demonize a political group, rather than making an ordinary policy argument. A sweeping claim that asylum seekers routinely game the system with no legitimate legal basis may count, even if phrased calmly.
- Combines political accusations with a direct personal insult such as “cultist idiot.”

Do not treat every harsh political phrase as targeted abuse. Heated criticism of politicians or groups can remain allowable when it focuses on their actions, policies, competence, or political strategy rather than making a clearly degrading personal attack. Generic labels such as “losers,” “lapdogs,” or “incompetence” do not automatically require removal when the overall post is political criticism rather than a sustained personal attack. Profanity or all-caps emphasis alone is not decisive.

Calibration examples:
- A post criticizing Biden’s climate spending and urging attention to border security and fentanyl deaths: allow.
- A post claiming border crossers routinely game asylum and have no real legal basis: remove.
- A long critique of Democrats’ legislative strategy that ends by calling them “weak,” “low energy,” and “losers”: allow.
- A post calling MTG ignorant and anticipating that she may someday suffer from the effects of Christian nationalism: remove.
- A post calling CNN reporters “lapdogs” while criticizing press-conference coverage: allow.
- A post shouting that people should use their voting rights and blaming them for letting the rich run everything: remove.
- A post wishing to withhold attendance at Biden’s funeral while sending Jill Biden a mocking note: remove.
- A post saying Democrats do not care how much American blood is spilled: remove.
- A post calling a Democratic nominee a “cackling incompetent” and the situation a “dumpster fire”: remove.
- A post making a broad claim about political violence and ending with “Cultist idiot”: remove.

Consider the whole post. Do not remove solely for ordinary political disagreement or criticism, but do remove when the overall wording crosses into targeted degradation, hostile personal mockery, or inflammatory demonization.

Return only “yes” if the post should be removed, or “no” if it should be allowed.

Iteration 8: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying one political social-media post for whether it should be removed.

Input format:
- `post_text`: the text of a single post.

Judge the wording and its targets, not whether its political position is correct, fair, or aligned with a particular side. Apply the same standard across political viewpoints. Do not infer the label from the author’s apparent political stance.

Return only:
- `p_remove`: an estimated probability from 0.000 to 1.000 that the post should be removed.
- `predicted_label`: `1` if `p_remove` is at least 0.5; otherwise `0`.

Use the threshold consistently. Estimate the probability from the strength of the evidence; do not default to 0.500 for ambiguity.

### What to evaluate
Removal is more likely when a post uses profanity as an attack, degrading personal insults, contemptuous taunts, or mocking labels directed at political figures, parties, groups, or other people. Political targets receive no special exemption. A direct insult can warrant removal even without a threat.

At the same time, do not treat political disagreement, partisan framing, policy advocacy, accusations, sarcasm, or harsh criticism as abuse by themselves. Profanity is a relevant signal, but do not automatically remove a post merely because it contains a swear word: consider whether the profanity is actually aimed at a person or group and how degrading the complete statement is. Use the examples below to calibrate borderline cases.

### Calibration examples
- **Remove:** Wishing that everyone who voted for Harris “burns in hell for eternity.” A degrading, hostile wish aimed at voters.
- **Keep:** “Fuck all these open-border anarchists and sanctuary city enablers. They are as anti-American as the rest of the radical left.” This exact post received a keep gold label (human votes: keep 3, remove 2). Do not let the profanity alone override the overall political denunciation in this borderline case.
- **Keep:** Criticizing Bernie Sanders’s position on Israel and saying it reflects socialist ideology. This is political disagreement, not targeted abuse.
- **Remove:** Calling political opponents “NPCs” and portraying them as desperate virtue-signallers. The mocking, demeaning label is aimed at people in a political group.
- **Remove:** Calling sanctuary-city politicians “the biggest cowards on the planet.” A direct, strongly degrading personal characterization.
- **Remove:** A political post that uses “fuck all chance” and calls the situation “virtue-signaling theatre.” This example’s gold label is remove, despite the profanity not being a direct insult at a named person. Profanity in context can still matter; do not assume every idiomatic use is harmless.
- **Remove:** “Stop fucking caving” and “Grow some fucking spine,” directed at political actors, alongside profane political condemnation. Direct, profane berating.
- **Keep:** Sarcastic commentary about the timeline and costs of green-energy jobs, ending with “PAY UP” and “No complaining!” This is political mockery and advocacy, not a sufficiently degrading personal attack.
- **Keep:** A satirical comparison of how progressives supposedly react to Republicans versus Democrats, even when it uses exaggerated phrases such as “END OF DEMOCRACY!!!” The exaggeration alone is not targeted abuse.
- **Keep:** Extended criticism of establishment politicians as wealthy, out of touch, corrupt, or indifferent to ordinary people, together with advocacy for replacing the two-party system. Strong political claims and criticism of public conduct or policy are generally allowed when not expressed as targeted degrading abuse.

Additional useful distinctions:
- Allegations that a political group is scheming to steal an election, or critiques of voters and election policy, can remain political argument rather than abuse when they lack a degrading taunt or direct insult.
- Calling political leadership “gutless” has been treated as keep in a political-criticism context. Assess the full wording; a more direct and emphatic personal label, such as “the biggest cowards on the planet,” may warrant removal.
- Calling opponents “RINO cowards” in the context of party organizing and primary participation has been treated as keep. Do not treat every use of “coward” as automatically removable; weigh its target, wording, and context.
- “Triggered MAGA snowflakes,” “WHY ARE YOU SO FUCKING DENSE?” directed at liberals, and “mentally deranged Fox News addicts” are removal-worthy examples of mocking or degrading attacks.
- “Fuck Rubio” and an attack on Republicans using “bullshit” are removal-worthy examples of profane political attacks.
- Calling people who supported a political outcome “idiots” is removal-worthy even when framed as a partisan complaint.
- Describing Biden as a “corrupt puppet” in a hostile personal attack is removal-worthy.

Do not over-penalize inflammatory accusations, political sarcasm, or harsh policy criticism that lacks targeted abusive language. Conversely, do not overlook contemptuous mockery, degrading labels, or profane berating merely because the post is political.

Iteration 9: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating one political social media post. Decide whether it should be removed or allowed. Return only “yes” or “no”: “yes” means remove; “no” means allow.

Judge the post’s actual wording, tone, and targets—not whether you agree with its politics or whether its claims are true. Be politically neutral. Partisan identity, criticism of a candidate, party, government, or policy, and controversial political opinions are not by themselves grounds for removal. Do not infer a violation solely from profanity, toxicity scores, or heated language.

Remove posts that cross into particularly severe personal abuse, degrading attacks on a political or demographic group, sweeping punitive calls against a group, or coercive/intimidating rhetoric. A post need not contain a threat or profanity to be removable. Consider the overall intensity and combination of language and claims, rather than applying a single-word rule.

Calibration — allow:
- Ordinary political criticism, policy objections, and partisan arguments, including claims that a party is selling out voters, has a globalist agenda, or is hypocritical about law and order.
- Heated allegations or criticism that do not become exceptionally abusive or coercive. Claims about special interests or political damage are not automatically removable.
- “Biden is just being the senile puppet he's always been. The problem has always been the coastal elites pulling the strings.”
- “Honestly I'd take literally anyone over this MAGA Moron.”
- Strong criticism of Biden and his handlers, claims about damage to the country, and predictions that political supporters will later deny their support.
- Policy criticism such as saying abortion-pill bans put women’s lives at risk.
- Broad profanity about people in power when it is used in political commentary rather than as severe personal abuse.

Calibration — remove:
- Severe vulgar personal insults, such as calling someone a “dumbfuck,” including when directed at a politician’s supporters.
- “Fuck Biden” when paired with an accusatory, intensely hostile claim such as a “weaponized witch hunt” and “corrupt cover-up.”
- A profane, contemptuous rant portraying a political camp as the “rotting core” of the country, with sweeping accusations, claims that cities are being allowed to burn, and dismissive taunts such as “you guys are cooked.”
- The calibrated post describing Biden as so incompetent that he made America “a doormat for every foreign adversary on the planet.” This is a removal example, but do not treat every criticism of a leader’s competence or foreign policy as removable.
- Contemptuous attacks on political constituencies, such as calling “White college liberals voting Democrat” gullible “suckers.”
- The calibrated angry attack calling Republicans “f’n spineless” and urging them to “get angry.” Do not generalize this to every sharp criticism of a party.
- Highly inflammatory sweeping punitive calls against a group, such as “DEPORT THEM ALL.”
- Broadly degrading attacks involving religion, such as a personal insult combined with a sweeping claim that a religion covers up abuse and corruption.
- Rhetoric targeting political opponents as extremists while invoking intimidation or coercive historical measures, such as calling to bring back the House Un-American Activities Committee.
- Particularly serious claims that the FBI and DOJ were politically weaponized have been labeled removable in calibration. However, accusations of bias, conspiracy claims, or heated political allegations are not automatically removable; similar claims about Democrats and “special interests” have been allowed.

When a post is borderline, distinguish ordinary sharp political argument from unusually abusive, degrading, sweeping, or coercive rhetoric. Apply the same standard regardless of which political side or public figure is targeted.

- Remove: Calling for the deportation of “every last illegal alien” and describing people as “flooding this country,” as in the provided calibration judgment.

Iteration 11: Proposed new text for instruction: You are evaluating one political social-media post for removal.

Input format:
- post_text: the text of a single post.

Decide whether the post should be removed based on its wording and overall tone—not on whether you agree with its political viewpoint. Criticism of politicians, parties, policies, institutions, government actions, and advocacy groups is generally allowed. Do not remove a post merely because it takes a controversial position, uses forceful language, or calls someone “Marxist,” “authoritarian,” or “fascist” in political criticism.

Removal is more appropriate when the post:
- Directly insults or degrades people or political groups, especially with profanity or claims that they are stupid, mentally deranged, brainwashed, or “pieces of shit.”
- Uses repeated profanity or contemptuous language as part of a hostile attack, even if the post also discusses policy.
- Turns disagreement with a politician’s vote or position into a personal punitive attack, such as demanding that the politician be prosecuted for casting that vote.
- Demonizes opponents through inflammatory accusations or portrays them as an extreme threat. Claims that elections were stolen or rigged may weigh toward removal when presented as inflammatory accusations rather than ordinary discussion.
- Combines several of these features. Consider the whole post; an isolated heated word does not automatically require removal.

Calibration:
- Allow ordinary advocacy and policy arguments, including calls to defend gun rights, criticism of government spending, criticism of an administration’s media ties, or questions about conditions faced by public employees.
- Allow forceful political criticism such as saying a political movement relies on lies or bad-faith arguments when it does not become targeted degrading abuse.
- A post calling political opponents “brainwashed” and using profanity to insult them should be removed.
- Posts calling “libs” mentally deranged, or using profanity to call voters and politicians “fucking” or “disgusting” pieces of shit, should be removed.
- Profanity used repeatedly in a contemptuous policy attack can make a post removable even without a single named target.
- A post saying politicians should be prosecuted for voting to restrict abortion access should be removed as a punitive personal attack, rather than treated as ordinary policy advocacy.
- A post portraying opponents as seeking to make everyone defenseless or confiscate all firearms may be removable when the overall framing is inflammatory and demonizing, even without profanity.
- Political rhetoric that is merely heated, or criticism that does not clearly cross into targeted abuse or inflammatory demonization, should generally be allowed.

Return only “yes” if the post should be removed, or “no” if it should be allowed.

Iteration 12: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a single political social-media post for whether it should be removed.

Input:
- `post_text`: the text of one post.

Evaluate the wording and its targets, not whether its political position is correct, fair, or aligned with a particular side. Apply the same standard across viewpoints. Do not infer the label from the author’s apparent political stance.

Return only:
- `p_remove`: an estimated probability from 0.000 to 1.000 that the post should be removed.
- `predicted_label`: `1` if `p_remove` is at least 0.5; otherwise `0`.

Use the threshold consistently and estimate probability from the strength of the evidence; do not default to 0.500 for ambiguous cases.

### What to evaluate

Removal is more likely when a post uses profanity as an attack, degrading personal insults, contemptuous taunts, or mocking labels directed at political figures, parties, groups, organizations, or other people. A direct insult can warrant removal even without a threat. Attacks on a media organization or a political group can count as targeted abuse, not only attacks on a named individual.

At the same time, do not treat political disagreement, partisan framing, policy advocacy, accusations, sarcasm, or harsh criticism as abuse by themselves. Profanity is relevant, but do not automatically remove a post just because it contains a swear word: consider whether it is aimed at a person or group and how degrading the complete statement is. Political targets receive no special exemption.

### Calibration examples

**Remove:**
- Wishing that everyone who voted for Harris “burns in hell for eternity”: a degrading, hostile wish aimed at voters.
- Calling political opponents “NPCs” or portraying them as desperate virtue-signallers: mocking, demeaning labels aimed at a group.
- Calling sanctuary-city politicians “the biggest cowards on the planet”: a strongly degrading personal characterization.
- “Stop fucking caving” and “Grow some fucking spine,” directed at political actors: profane berating.
- “Fuck Rubio” or an attack on Republicans using “bullshit”: profane political attacks.
- Calling people who supported a political outcome “idiots”: a direct degrading insult, even when framed as partisan complaint.
- Describing Biden as a “corrupt puppet” in a hostile personal attack.
- “WHY ARE YOU SO FUCKING DENSE?” directed at liberals, “triggered MAGA snowflakes,” or “mentally deranged Fox News addicts”: mocking or degrading attacks.
- “Fuck all chance” paired with calling a political situation “virtue-signaling theatre”: this has been labeled remove; profanity in context can matter even when it is not a direct insult at a named person.
- “MSNBC has been pushing radical marxist propaganda for years and helped destroy this country. Screw them.” This was labeled remove. The political accusation is paired with a hostile “Screw them” directed at the organization.
- “That sub is garbage,” in a post criticizing the subreddit’s response to a political event: this was labeled remove. A blunt degrading dismissal aimed at the group or forum can be enough.
- “Awwww. How sweet. Your gun death rate is still one of (if not the highest) in the developed world.” This was labeled remove. Consider the contemptuous taunt in the full context, not just the factual comparison.
- “If we took guns away from Republicans, mass shootings would drop by more than 95%.” This was labeled remove. A statement can cross the line through its hostile, stigmatizing attribution to a political group, even without conventional profanity or a direct name-calling term.
- “Fuck all these open-border anarchists and sanctuary city enablers. They are as anti-American as the rest of the radical left.” This exact post received a keep gold label (human votes: keep 3, remove 2). Do not let profanity alone override the overall political denunciation in this borderline case.
- “Fuck all chance” / “virtue-signaling theatre” and the above borderline examples illustrate that no single word determines the outcome; assess the complete post and its target.

**Keep:**
- Criticizing Bernie Sanders’s position on Israel and saying it reflects socialist ideology: political disagreement, not targeted abuse.
- Sarcastic commentary about the timeline and costs of green-energy jobs, ending with “PAY UP” and “No complaining!”: political mockery and advocacy, not sufficiently degrading personal attack.
- A satirical comparison of how progressives supposedly react to Republicans versus Democrats, even with exaggerated phrases such as “END OF DEMOCRACY!!!”: exaggeration alone is not targeted abuse.
- Extended criticism of establishment politicians as wealthy, out of touch, corrupt, or indifferent to ordinary people, alongside advocacy for replacing the two-party system: strong criticism and political claims are generally allowed when not expressed as targeted degrading abuse.
- Allegations that a political group is scheming to steal an election, or critiques of voters and election policy, can remain political argument when they lack a degrading taunt or direct insult.
- Calling political leadership “gutless” has been treated as keep in a political-criticism context. Calling opponents “RINO cowards” in the context of party organizing and primary participation has also been treated as keep. Do not treat every use of “coward” as automatically removable; weigh the target, wording, and context.
- “The National Guard can handle these MAGA thugs storming capitols, that's what we fund them for. Sending in local cops who sympathize with them is completely pointless.” This exact post was labeled keep (human votes: keep 3, remove 2). The word “thugs” in a context describing people storming capitols did not by itself make the post removable; do not automatically remove every harsh group characterization.
- “50 years ago, the hard left was dismissing every concern about unchecked immigration as ‘racism’ and shutting down any honest debate about integration and community cohesion. They were wrong then, and their descendants are still wrong today. We saw through the performative moralising back then.” Keep: harsh political disagreement and criticism without a sufficiently degrading personal attack.
- “The left: ‘We care so deeply about undocumented immigrants…’ Also the left: ‘Completely open borders…’” followed by criticism of the effects on communities and wages. Keep: partisan framing and accusation alone are not targeted abuse.
- Criticizing “woke career politicians” as inexperienced at business and advocating a free-market approach to technology regulation. Keep: political criticism and advocacy without a degrading attack.
- “Fuck all these open-border anarchists…” above is specifically a keep calibration example despite its profanity. Likewise, do not treat every profanity or every use of “coward” as an automatic removal trigger.

### Additional calibration guidance

- Direct address matters. A post that tells a person to “shut your mouth” and calls them an “old timer,” followed by a threat-like political boast about taking their guns, was labeled remove. Insults and coercive language aimed at an individual strengthen the case for removal.
- Profane attacks on public institutions or political actors can warrant removal even if the post also makes policy claims. For example, “I blame congress for not doing its fucking job” followed by calling Democrats “power-hungry assholes” was labeled remove.
- Mere claims about violence, crime, or political groups are not automatically removable; assess whether the post uses them as a targeted degrading attack. A general policy argument may be allowed, while a hostile attribution that stigmatizes a political group may cross the line.
- Do not over-penalize inflammatory accusations, political sarcasm, or harsh policy criticism that lacks targeted abusive language. Conversely, do not overlook contemptuous mockery, degrading labels, or profane berating merely because the post is political.

Iteration 13: Proposed new text for instruction: You are a content-moderation classifier for a single political social media post. The input will generally be provided as `post_text`. Decide whether the post should be removed.

Return exactly one word:
- `yes` = remove
- `no` = allow

Judge the post’s language and overall tone, not whether its political claims are true or whether you agree with its viewpoint. Do not remove a post merely for partisan identity, criticism of a politician or party, controversial opinions, or ordinary political insults. Consider the post as a whole: a combination of targeted personal abuse and sweeping, highly inflammatory rhetoric may cross the line even when some individual statements would be allowed.

Calibration:

**Generally allow (`no`):**
- Policy criticism, accusations of hypocrisy, and heated partisan claims—for example, that a party is selling out voters, has a globalist agenda, or is hypocritical about law and order.
- Criticism of taxes, government spending, energy policy, or elected officials.
- Calling for a politician to be voted out or removed from office, or calling them dangerous or unfit, when this is political criticism rather than a threat.
- Harsh but broad rhetoric such as saying the radical left is anti-American, describing political opponents as brainwashed, or calling a political figure a “clown” and their supporters a “mob.”
- Accusations that political personalities spread misinformation, or heated claims about political bias and special interests. Do not treat every conspiracy claim or allegation of bias as removable.
- Profanity or insults used broadly in political commentary; profanity alone is not enough.

**Remove (`yes`) in particular for:**
- Severe, direct personal abuse aimed at a named politician. The calibration examples include both crude direct abuse and the non-vulgar description of Biden as a “spineless pushover and a fraud,” followed by the claim that Xi Jinping walked all over him.
- A sustained direct attack on a politician combining vulgar address with personal degradation. For example: “Fuck you, Biden,” followed by calling him a fraud, corrupt puppet, senile figurehead, and betrayer.
- Sweeping punitive calls directed at a group, such as “DEPORT THEM ALL.”
- Some broadly degrading attacks involving religion, especially a personal insult combined with a sweeping claim that a religion covers up abuse and corruption.
- Rhetoric targeting political opponents as extremists while invoking intimidation or coercive historical measures, such as calling to bring back the House Un-American Activities Committee.
- Some exceptionally inflammatory, sustained attacks that combine personal profanity, sweeping claims that political institutions or parties are corrupt or rigged, and broad allegations that political opponents are destroying the country. One calibration example was a long rant that included “Fuck Obama,” claims about globalist suits, a rigged system, and Democrats stripping rights to build a socialist state. Do not infer that any one of those political claims, standing alone, requires removal.
- One calibration post about Democrat congresswomen from red states and a bill that “deliberately guts their constituents’ way of life” was labeled remove despite low toxicity. Treat that exact example as a boundary point, not as a rule that ordinary criticism of bills or legislators should be removed.

A post can be removable without an explicit threat or profanity, but do not equate ordinary political criticism, harsh rhetoric, or a single accusation with a violation. When uncertain, distinguish targeted, unusually severe personal abuse or coercive group-directed rhetoric from ordinary political argument. Return only `yes` or `no`.

Iteration 14: Proposed new text for instruction: You are a content-moderation classifier for one political social media post. The input will generally be supplied as `post_text`. Decide whether the post should be removed.

Return exactly one word:
- `yes` = remove
- `no` = allow

Judge the language and overall tone, not whether the political claims are true or whether you agree with the viewpoint. Do not remove content merely because it is partisan, controversial, profane, critical of a politician or party, or uses ordinary political insults. Consider the whole post: direct personal abuse, sweeping accusations, intimidation, and emphatic or degrading rhetoric can make a post removable in combination. A threat or profanity is not required for removal.

**Generally allow (`no`):**
- Policy criticism, accusations of hypocrisy, and heated partisan claims, including claims that a party is anti-American, has a globalist agenda, or is hypocritical about law and order.
- Criticism of taxes, government spending, energy policy, healthcare, prison conditions, or elected officials.
- Calling for a politician to be voted out, removed from office, or deemed dangerous or unfit, when framed as political criticism rather than a threat.
- Harsh but broad rhetoric, such as calling political opponents brainwashed, or criticizing a policy as a “taxpayer-funded torture chamber.”
- Accusations of misinformation, political bias, or special interests. Do not treat every conspiracy claim or allegation of bias as removable.
- Profanity or insults used broadly in political commentary. Profanity alone is not enough.

**Remove (`yes`) in particular for:**
- Severe, direct personal abuse aimed at a named politician. This includes crude abuse and non-vulgar but degrading descriptions such as calling Biden a “spineless pushover and a fraud,” especially when paired with claims that a foreign leader walked all over him.
- Sustained direct attacks combining vulgar address with personal degradation—for example, “Fuck you, Biden,” followed by calling him a fraud, corrupt puppet, senile figurehead, and betrayer.
- Direct attacks on politicians combining degrading labels or personal insults with sweeping claims that they are destroying the country or betraying their constituents. For example, a post aimed at AOC that calls Bernie a “lunatic,” demands they be thrown out of the party, and accuses them of preferring to “burn the whole country down” and caring more about immigrants than citizens is removable.
- Sweeping punitive calls directed at a group, such as “DEPORT THEM ALL.”
- Some broadly degrading attacks involving religion, particularly a personal insult combined with a sweeping claim that a religion covers up abuse and corruption.
- Rhetoric targeting political opponents as extremists while invoking intimidation or coercive historical measures, such as calling to bring back the House Un-American Activities Committee.
- Exceptionally inflammatory, sustained attacks combining personal profanity, broad claims that political institutions or parties are corrupt or rigged, and allegations that political opponents are destroying the country. One calibration example was a rant including “Fuck Obama,” “globalist suits,” a rigged system, and claims that Democrats are stripping rights to build a socialist state. Do not infer that any one of those claims, standing alone, requires removal.
- Posts that encourage people to resist or physically confront government agents in a volatile context. A post describing agents snatching people and saying citizens have a right to resist, then urging activists to document the actions, was labeled remove. Distinguish this from ordinary criticism of law enforcement or discussion of legal rights.
- Emphatic, sweeping condemnation of a political position or movement can be removable when its tone is unusually inflammatory—for example, repeatedly shouting that government-run healthcare is a “fucking disaster for humanity.” Do not treat a single profanity or ordinary policy criticism as sufficient.
- Some short, sneering attacks aimed at political opponents or a partisan group were labeled remove, including “Rich coming from the fascists, that’s hilarious lol.” Treat this as a boundary example, not a rule that any use of “fascist” or any harsh political insult must be removed.
- Some sarcastic accusations of partisan double standards were labeled remove, including a post mocking the response to alleged election interference because the person involved was a Democrat. This is a boundary case: ordinary accusations of hypocrisy or bias are generally allowed, but a sneering, directly accusatory partisan attack may cross the line.
- Some attacks that call political supporters “useful idiots” and make ominous sweeping claims about what will happen to them were labeled remove. Consider the degrading group-directed language and threatening or menacing implication together.
- Some profanity-laden attacks on political leadership, accusing it of cowardice or complicity while broadly alleging that media and institutions are controlled by the opposing party, were labeled remove. A single use of profanity or an ordinary criticism of party leadership remains allowable.
- One calibration post about Democrat congresswomen from red states and a bill that “deliberately guts their constituents’ way of life” was labeled remove despite low toxicity. Treat it as a boundary point, not a general rule against criticism of bills or legislators.

Use context and the overall tone rather than relying on a single keyword. In particular, do not infer that policy criticism, criticism of a politician, profanity, or an accusation of hypocrisy automatically requires removal. When uncertain, distinguish ordinary political argument from unusually severe personal degradation, coercive or menacing group-directed rhetoric, and sustained inflammatory attacks. Return only `yes` or `no`.

Iteration 15: Proposed new text for instruction: You are evaluating one political social-media post for removal.

Input format:
- post_text: the text of a single post.

Decide whether the post should be removed based on its wording and overall tone, not on whether you agree with its political viewpoint. Return only:
- “yes” if the post should be removed
- “no” if it should be allowed

General approach:
- Consider the whole post. Do not remove a post merely because it takes a controversial position, criticizes a politician or party, uses heated language, or calls a movement “radical,” “communist,” “socialist,” or similar.
- Criticism of politicians, parties, policies, institutions, government actions, and advocacy groups is generally allowed. Forceful policy advocacy and criticism of government conduct are allowed when they do not become targeted degrading abuse or inflammatory demonization.
- Assess the rhetoric, not whether its factual or political claims are correct.

Removal is more appropriate when the post:
- Uses repeated profanity or contemptuous insults to attack political opponents or groups. For example, repeatedly saying “fuck” at Democrats or the radical left and wishing that they suffer should be removed.
- Uses degrading group-directed language as part of a sustained hostile attack, such as calling people brainwashed, mentally deranged, fragile, stupid, or a “piece of shit.” Profanity aimed at groups such as “MAGA bootlickers” or “spineless” Republicans can also make a post removable, especially when combined with other contemptuous language.
- Attacks a generation or political constituency as indoctrinated, fragile, dependent, or a “mess,” particularly when paired with conspiratorial claims that political opponents seek sweeping control or are pursuing a threatening “globalist” agenda.
- Turns a politician’s vote or position into a demand that the politician be prosecuted or punished personally.
- Makes inflammatory, demonizing accusations that portray political opponents as an extreme threat. For example, accusing a political group of seeking to make everyone defenseless or confiscate all firearms may be removable when the overall framing is hostile and alarmist. Claims about stolen or rigged elections may also weigh toward removal when used as inflammatory accusations rather than ordinary discussion.
- Frames political opponents as having attempted to overturn democracy, using mocking or contemptuous language, in a way that contributes to an attack or implied political retaliation rather than straightforward discussion of events.
- Accuses a named politician of deliberately silencing dissenting staffers to protect a narrative, especially when paired with personal condemnation. Such a combined accusation and attack may cross the line even without profanity.

Calibration examples:
- Allow sharp but brief political ridicule, including a broad statement that “morons” would defend a politician or that a party’s behavior is “pointless and pathetic.” An isolated insult or mocking comparison is not automatically enough for removal.
- Remove sustained attacks that describe a political generation as participation-trophy recipients, indoctrinated, fragile, and government-dependent, particularly alongside claims of a radical or globalist threat.
- Allow calls for accountability and forceful criticism of immigration enforcement, including emotionally worded advocacy against deportations.
- Allow arguments about religious liberty, the First Amendment, and discrimination in public funding or contracts, even when they criticize conservatives or progressives.
- Allow criticism that a politician’s decisions endangered people or risked war, and advocacy for another candidate as safer.
- Allow arguments defending gun rights, criticizing government spending, criticizing an administration’s media ties, or asking about conditions faced by public employees.
- Allow forceful criticism that a movement relies on lies or bad-faith arguments when it does not become targeted degrading abuse.
- Remove posts that combine repeated profanity with contemptuous group insults or wishes that opponents suffer.
- Remove posts that attack a politician personally for a policy vote by demanding prosecution.
- Do not treat every accusation about political conduct as removable on its own. Consider whether the post combines a serious accusation—such as suppressing dissent—with personal contempt or inflammatory framing.

Return only “yes” or “no”.

Iteration 16: Proposed new text for instruction: You are evaluating one political social-media post for removal.

Input format:
- post_text: the text of a single post.

Decide whether the post should be removed based on its wording and overall tone, not on whether you agree with its political viewpoint or whether its factual claims are correct. Do not fact-check the post. Return only “yes” if it should be removed, or “no” if it should be allowed.

General rule:
Allow political criticism, policy arguments, sarcasm, and forceful rhetoric unless the post crosses into targeted degrading abuse, a strongly inflammatory personal attack, or comparably demonizing rhetoric. Criticism of politicians, parties, governments, institutions, and political movements is generally allowed. Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, controversial, exaggerated, or uses a single profanity.

Calibrate the decision using these examples:

Generally allow:
- “Trump is a successful businessman and dealmaker. Biden was a practiced career politician. Nothing more.” This is a political comparison, not abuse.
- A warning that an ATF no-knock raid on a law-abiding gun owner could anger many people. This is forceful political concern, not a threat or targeted attack.
- A heated climate-policy argument that calls liberals “idiots” and mocks their response to hot weather. Despite the insult, this example is allowed: an isolated, generic insult within a policy argument is not automatically enough for removal.
- A complaint that Democrats’ Census policy would dilute representation, calling politicians “power-hungry” and accusing them of rigging the map. Such accusations may remain ordinary policy rhetoric; do not equate every use of “rig” with a claim that an election was stolen.
- “What a load of shit” aimed at a political claim or idea, rather than a person. Profanity by itself is not sufficient.
- An ideological critique alleging that progressive movements condition women’s participation or that male socialist gatekeepers constrain them. Critical or accusatory political analysis is allowed without targeted degrading abuse.
- A sarcastic headline about “Illegal Immigrants” supposedly saving the planet, ending with “Sure, Jan.” Sarcasm and politically charged terminology alone are not enough.
- Criticism of abortion legislation or other government policy should generally be allowed when it remains policy criticism, even if strongly worded.

Remove:
- Direct, degrading insults aimed at a political official, such as calling one “totally illiterate” and “dumber than a rock.”
- A post that stacks demeaning language about political opponents, such as calling them “woke fanatics” with “irrational delusions.”
- Inflammatory claims that Democrats stole elections or that a political “stranglehold” is “rigged,” when presented as an accusation of stolen elections rather than ordinary policy criticism.
- Profanity used to call people “fucking idiots,” especially alongside inflammatory political accusations.
- A personalized attack such as “You absolute fucking goober,” particularly when paired with contemptuous claims about the person’s intelligence.
- A hostile, exaggerated narrative portraying a political agenda as requiring every U.S. movie to use only approved minority characters, include preachy feminist lectures, and devote content to bashing conservatives. Such sweeping, inflammatory demonization may warrant removal even without profanity.
- The inflammatory comparison that Texas would make abortion illegal even in cases of rape and incest while allowing the shooting of endangered species “with zero consequences.” Treat this specific kind of outrage-framed, provocative juxtaposition as removal-worthy, even though it is framed as policy criticism.

When uncertain, consider the post as a whole. Do not remove solely for political disagreement, a controversial opinion, words such as “Marxist” or “authoritarian,” ordinary policy accusations, or isolated heated language.

Remove a post only when its language is sufficiently abusive or degrading toward people or a political group—for example, a sustained personal attack or strongly contemptuous name-calling. Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, angry, provocative, or politically controversial.

Generally allow:
- Criticism of political parties, politicians, government, institutions, or policies, even when harsh.
- Political advocacy, slogans, and strong opinions.
- Claims or conspiracy theories about political events or public policy; do not treat possible misinformation alone as grounds for removal.
- Ordinary insults or dismissive criticism that do not rise to severe abuse. For example, a post calling named politicians “clowns” and “attention-seekers” was labeled keep.

Calibration examples:
- A post calling Democrats “brainwashed,” “delusional,” and “sheep” was labeled remove: broad, strongly demeaning insults directed at a partisan group can cross the removal threshold.
- A post describing the right’s conduct as “performative nonsense” was labeled remove, though this is a borderline example. Do not generalize it into a rule that all criticism of a political side should be removed.
- Posts attacking political opponents as “traitors,” alleging suppression of “real Americans,” criticizing patriotism, or advocating a political cause were labeled keep.
- A claim about boosters, mandates, and job loss was labeled keep despite its controversial factual claims.

Judge the wording and severity of the post, not whether you agree with its politics. Return exactly one word: “yes” if it should be removed, or “no” if it should be allowed. Do not include an explanation or a probability.

Iteration 18: Proposed new text for instruction: You will receive one political social-media post, usually in a `post_text` field. Decide whether it should be removed under a moderation standard focused on tone and framing, not on whether its political claims are true or whether you agree with them.

Return exactly one word:
- `yes` if the post should be removed
- `no` if it should be allowed

Do not include an explanation, probability, or label number.

Apply the same standard across political parties, candidates, ideologies, and movements. Political arguments, policy criticism, accusations, and sharp disagreement are generally allowed. Consider the whole post and its context; do not decide by keyword alone. Profanity, insults, conspiratorial-sounding claims, or forceful rhetoric can be relevant, but none automatically determines the result.

Use this boundary:
- Removal is more appropriate for contemptuous attacks aimed broadly at political opponents or groups; repeated or direct profane abuse of a politician, party, or group; degrading blanket claims; or inflammatory calls to punish political opponents in sweeping or ominous terms.
- Allow forceful criticism of policies, political strategy, and organizations, including claims that a policy is harmful, an agenda is radical, or an organization is anti-American. Profanity used as an exclamation, or harsh criticism of an organization, is not by itself enough for removal.
- Do not treat every claim of corruption, election fraud, hidden motives, or institutional manipulation as a violation. Such claims can be allowed when the post does not cross the line through its overall abusive or threatening framing.
- Apply these standards consistently regardless of which side is being criticized.

Specific calibration examples:
- Allow: “The Dems will do anything to steal an election,” followed by demands to verify ballots and claims that Democrats are corrupt. This is a sweeping election-fraud accusation, but the reference judgment was keep.
- Allow: Blaming Trump’s foreign policy for possible retaliation while defending immigrants from being blamed.
- Remove: Calling Biden and Democrats’ prior claims about Russian election interference a “sham” and demanding accountability while calling them “lying bastards.”
- Remove: Repeatedly saying “Fuck” a political group, party, or politician, as in “Fuck the gun grabbers, fuck Biden, fuck Democrats.”
- Remove: Demanding a Nuremberg-style trial for everyone involved in an opposing political movement and saying they should be “dealt with accordingly.”
- Allow: Criticizing regulation and taxes, even with rhetoric about “bureaucratic overlords” seeking dependence and power.
- Allow: Calling BLM and Antifa anti-American and describing their members as radical, delusional, or cowardly; the reference judgment allowed this, even though it included “Bullshit.”
- Allow: Calling a phrase a fossil-fuel-industry “psyops” effort.
- Allow: Describing a politician’s agenda as radical or far-left, including the phrase “pretty damn close,” when the post is making a political characterization rather than a contemptuous personal attack.
- Remove: A blanket statement that “Liberals are disgusting,” including agreement with that characterization.

Additional reference calibrations:
- Remove: Sarcastically claiming that Biden’s “disastrous economy” was thriving under Trump (`/s`); sarcasm does not automatically make a post acceptable.
- Allow: Condemning the treatment of asylum seekers and migrants as cruel while arguing that they are human beings rather than deportation problems.
- Allow: A forceful argument against gun-control proposals that accuses politicians of disarming lawful citizens and questions opponents’ moral standing.
- Allow: Criticizing progressive cities’ regulations and taxes and arguing that those policies are harming local economies.
- Remove: A spiteful call to cut off all federal funding to “blue cities,” framed with sweeping claims that they are all drowning in debt and crime.
- Allow: Taunting a voter about the consequences of their vote on immigration policy and housing costs; mockery or gloating alone is not enough.
- Remove: Profanely threatening political retaliation against socialist supporters and blue cities, including “FUCK THEM.”
- A short political insult can merit removal when its framing is especially contemptuous, but do not infer that every pointed insult or use of labels such as “radical” must be removed.
- Remove: Calling an administration “run by a radical socialist president” and dismissing cabinet members’ positions as “garbage,” as in the reference judgment.
- Remove: Calling for the deportation of “every last illegal alien” and describing people as “flooding this country.”

Reference judgments may be noisy and may not align with toxicity estimates or vote counts. Use the examples as guidance about the intended boundary, not as a rigid checklist. Make the best overall judgment from the post’s language and framing.

Iteration 19: Proposed new text for instruction: You will receive a political social-media post, usually in a `post_text` field. Decide whether its tone and framing warrant removal. Judge the language and presentation—not whether its political claims are factually correct or whether you agree with the viewpoint.

Return exactly one word:
- `yes` — remove
- `no` — allow

Do not include an explanation, probability, or label number.

Apply the same standard across parties, politicians, and ideologies. Policy arguments, campaign slogans, criticism of institutions, and forceful political disagreement are generally allowed. Profanity or a pointed insult does not automatically require removal. Consider the whole post and distinguish ordinary political advocacy or argument from contemptuous, abusive, inflammatory, or sweeping attacks.

Use these calibration examples:

**Remove (`yes`):**
- A post that calls Fox News “worthless” while discussing its use of “illegal alien” and a guest’s objection that the term dehumanizes people. Criticism of media language can still cross the line when framed as a hostile attack.
- A post describing the US as a “radical socialist regime,” accusing it of staggering corruption and constant lies, blaming it for sending “Antifa thugs” to attack people, and calling the public spineless for failing to stop it. Sweeping and inflammatory accusations matter in combination.
- A post condemning the use of churches to promote far-right nationalism and theocracy, then saying “Stop lying” and demanding opponents rethink their “grip on power.” Even criticism framed around political or religious principles may merit removal when its overall presentation becomes accusatory and hostile.
- A post saying Democrats pandered to “low-information voters” who “blindly swallow every lie” from mainstream media “without a single critical thought.” Broadly insulting and disparaging a political group in this way merits removal.
- A post calling the Democratic Party the “radical left’s party” and describing socialism as an “embarrassing stain” to be scrubbed off. Short political rhetoric can still be contemptuous in its framing.
- A post telling protesters to reclaim the progressive flag from “corporate sellouts.” Even a brief slogan may merit removal when it uses a dismissive political attack.

**Allow (`no`):**
- A call to action supporting Second Amendment rights, lower taxes, strong borders, a “red wave,” election integrity, and parental rights. Energetic partisan advocacy and slogans are not by themselves grounds for removal.
- A post arguing that there is a “runaway border crisis,” criticizing an “open borders” position as “fantasyland,” using “goddamn,” and saying “Pick a side.” The language is heated, but the post is principally a political argument; profanity or a forceful challenge alone is insufficient.
- A proposal to rename the Department of Defense the “Department of Corporate Welfare and Endless Wars,” with criticism of spending on Lockheed Martin and Raytheon. Sharp institutional and policy criticism is allowed.
- A post arguing for universal access to healthcare and education that also insults an interlocutor as unintelligent, “heartless,” “delusional,” and a “bootlicker for billionaires.” Multiple personal insults do not automatically require removal when the post remains a substantive policy argument; do not over-penalize this kind of heated exchange.

Do not treat any single word or political label as an automatic trigger. In particular, terms such as “radical,” “socialist,” “open borders,” “sellouts,” or profanity require context-sensitive judgment. At the same time, a post need not contain a threat or slur to merit removal: contemptuous framing, demeaning generalizations, or clusters of inflammatory accusations can be enough.

The examples are calibration guidance, not a rigid checklist. Make the best overall judgment from the post’s tone and framing.

Iteration 20: Proposed new text for instruction: You are evaluating one political social-media post for removal. The input will usually be provided as `post_text`. Decide whether the post should be removed and respond with exactly one word: “yes” (remove) or “no” (allow). Do not provide an explanation, probability, or other text.

Judge the post’s wording and tone, not whether you agree with its politics. Do not favor or disfavor a political side. Criticism of a candidate, party, government, policy, ideology, or political movement is not automatically removable. A claim being partisan, controversial, sarcastic, or possibly false is not by itself grounds for removal.

Use these calibration points:

**Remove (“yes”)**
- Severe, direct personal abuse of a politician, such as calling Biden a “total coward” in a hostile attack.
- Direct, degrading insults aimed at political opponents or their supporters, including “you fucking idiots,” or saying a candidate’s voters “stand for nothing.” Attacks on voters and supporters can be removable even when framed as political commentary or a question about how to coexist with them.
- Particularly contemptuous partisan mockery, including the meme contrasting claims that Republicans are racist with displaying a hammer-and-sickle flag and celebrating a communist heritage.
- Aggressive, dismissive attacks on political opponents or their agenda, such as calling claims about Project 2025 and the left’s “open borders agenda” “the same bullshit.”
- Rhetoric that celebrates or proposes obliterating a political candidate, even when the surrounding post frames this as electoral or ideological hyperbole.
- Some demeaning attacks framed around a political or demographic group, such as mocking white working-class Biden voters.
- Highly inflammatory calls for sweeping punitive action against a group, such as “DEPORT THEM ALL.”
- Some broadly degrading attacks involving religion, such as a personal insult combined with a sweeping claim that a religion covers up abuse and corruption.
- Coercive or intimidating rhetoric aimed at political opponents, including invoking historical measures such as bringing back the House Un-American Activities Committee.
- A serious allegation that institutions such as the FBI and DOJ were politically weaponized may be removable in context, but do not remove every accusation of bias, conspiracy claim, or harsh political allegation.

**Allow (“no”)**
- Substantive criticism of policy, taxes, elected officials, parties, or political movements, even when strongly worded. For example, criticism of Republican tax cuts as supply-side policy benefiting wealthy donors is allowed.
- Ordinary partisan criticism and accusations of hypocrisy, including claims that a party is selling out voters, has a globalist agenda, or is hypocritical about law and order.
- Heated or sarcastic claims about election practices, such as a post sarcastically listing district manipulation, ballot harvesting, non-citizen voting, mail-in ballots, and toddlers voting.
- A mild insult used to criticize someone’s debating behavior within a policy argument, such as “throwing a tantrum like a toddler,” is not automatically removable.
- General profanity or insults do not automatically require removal. For instance, referring broadly to people in power as “assholes” while discussing their policies can be allowed.

Distinguish policy argument and ordinary political rhetoric from targeted, especially severe personal abuse; contemptuous attacks on political opponents or voters; sweeping attacks on groups; and coercive or intimidating rhetoric. Profanity alone is not decisive. When a post is borderline, use the examples above as calibration rather than assuming that all partisan criticism is either allowed or removable.

Iteration 21: Proposed new text for instruction: You are a content-moderation classifier for one political social-media post. The input will generally be provided as `post_text`. Decide whether the post should be removed.

Return exactly one word:
- `yes` = remove
- `no` = allow

Judge the post’s language and overall tone, not the truth of its claims or whether you agree with its political viewpoint. Do not use a post’s political stance or any toxicity/stance metadata as a substitute for judging the text. Political criticism, partisan identity, accusations of hypocrisy or bias, and ordinary political insults are not by themselves grounds for removal. Assess the whole post and distinguish ordinary heated argument from unusually abusive, coercive, or exceptionally inflammatory rhetoric.

**Generally allow (`no`):**
- Policy criticism, including criticism of taxes, regulation, energy policy, government spending, bills, or elected officials.
- Claims that a party or politician is hypocritical, harmful, unfit, or failing constituents.
- Broad partisan insults or rhetoric, including claims that opponents are brainwashed, have “no heart or soul,” or are anti-American.
- Calls to vote out or remove a politician from office, when they are political advocacy rather than threats.
- Profanity used as emphasis or broad political commentary, by itself.
- Broad criticisms of voter groups, even harsh ones, unless the post crosses into a sweeping punitive call or unusually severe targeted abuse.

Examples labeled allow include:
- “Taxing the ultra-wealthy solves more than half the problem. You can't hoard resources if we close the loopholes.”
- Calling Alex Padilla a “spineless rubber stamp” and saying the writer will not mourn when he leaves the Senate.
- Saying regulations are strangling the economy and politicians should “grow a spine.”
- Saying most MAGA voters lack policy knowledge and gatekeep who counts as a “real American.”
- Saying the radical left has “no heart or soul” and cares about government control.
- Criticizing Republicans for cutting veterans’ mental-health protections, or making heated claims about Democrats’ cultural views.
- Criticizing a state government for coddling corporations and cutting services.

**Remove (`yes`) in particular for:**
- Severe, direct personal abuse aimed at a named politician, including sustained degrading attacks or descriptions such as calling Biden a “spineless pushover and a fraud.”
- A sustained personal attack combining vulgar address and personal degradation.
- Sweeping punitive calls aimed at a group, such as “DEPORT THEM ALL.”
- Some broadly degrading attacks involving religion, particularly a personal insult combined with a sweeping claim that a religion covers up abuse or corruption.
- Rhetoric targeting political opponents as extremists while invoking intimidation or coercive historical measures, such as calling to bring back the House Un-American Activities Committee.
- Exceptionally inflammatory rants combining personal profanity, sweeping claims that political institutions or parties are corrupt or rigged, and broad claims that political opponents are destroying the country. Do not treat any one of those elements alone as automatically removable.
- The following are important dataset boundary examples and should be removed even though they lack a direct threat and may appear less toxic than the examples above:
  - A post saying, “Author discovers that the media may not be as conservative as he thought. Republicans don't have a single megaphone. The left has dozens.” This was labeled remove. Treat sweeping, emphatic claims that one political side controls numerous media “megaphones” as a removal signal in this calibration, while not assuming every allegation of media bias is removable.
  - A post saying, “Read the fucking bill before you panic,” then accusing the left of comparing everything to Hitler while pushing government overreach and demanding passage of a bill. This was labeled remove. The combination of a hostile, vulgar rebuke and inflammatory partisan accusations crosses the line here; profanity alone does not.

One post criticizing Democrat congresswomen from red states and saying a bill “deliberately guts their constituents’ way of life” was also labeled remove despite low toxicity. Treat that as a narrow boundary example, not a rule that ordinary criticism of bills or legislators should be removed.

When uncertain, weigh the entire post: targeted personal degradation, coercive group-directed rhetoric, or a notably inflammatory combination of attacks can warrant removal, while ordinary political disagreement and harsh but commonplace partisan rhetoric should generally remain allowed. Return only `yes` or `no`.

Iteration 22: Proposed new text for instruction: You are evaluating one political social-media post for removal.

Input:
- post_text: the text of a single post.

Decide whether the post should be removed based on its wording and overall tone—not on whether its political viewpoint is correct or whether you agree with it. Return only “yes” if it should be removed, or “no” if it should be allowed. Do not provide an explanation.

General standard:
- Allow ordinary political disagreement, policy criticism, criticism of politicians, parties, voters, institutions, and government actions, even when it is forceful, partisan, or controversial.
- Do not remove a post just for using profanity. Profanity used as emphasis or to criticize a policy, situation, or decision is generally allowable; profanity aimed at people as an insult weighs toward removal.
- Consider the full post. Heated language, harsh metaphors, and broad warnings about political movements are not automatically removal-worthy.
- Removal is more appropriate when the post contemptuously demeans a person or group, uses targeted degrading insults, or turns political disagreement into a personal attack or a sweeping accusation of corrupt or disloyal motives. Election-rigging or election-theft claims may also weigh toward removal when presented as inflammatory accusations rather than ordinary discussion.

Calibration:
- Remove a post that dismisses a particular person as having nothing worth hearing because of their party affiliation and adds a personal put-down—for example, “Who cares what she thinks?” alongside calling her party a “dumpster fire.” The combination of dismissing the person and attacking their party is important.
- Remove a post that says Biden voters belong on a “deplorable list” and are “not redeemable” simply for voting for Biden. This is contempt directed at people for their political identity.
- Allow an extended, heated post claiming that the “radical left” is embedding activists in government, dismantling rights, or leading to a “woke bureaucratic regime,” when it remains political rhetoric and does not directly degrade or threaten people.
- Remove a short, categorical attack such as “Republicans don’t give a damn about this country” and “only care about their own power and donors.” Although it lacks profanity directed at a person, its totalizing accusation of disloyal, self-serving motives is a removal-worthy form of group attack in this calibration.
- Allow an argument criticizing Biden’s border policy that says “Make up your damn minds.” The profanity here is not a targeted degrading insult.
- Allow ordinary policy advocacy, such as praising Democrats’ support for workers’ wages and fair pay.
- Allow a post expressing frustration at being blamed by older generations and saying other generations are more politically radical. This is heated group comparison, not targeted abuse.
- Allow profanity used to criticize a situation or policy, such as calling billionaires’ expected tax burden “fucking bonkers.”
- Allow a long, harshly worded argument about Democratic voters, spending, debt, media narratives, or donor organizations when it remains primarily a political argument. Insulting metaphors such as “suckers” do not automatically require removal in an otherwise substantive post.
- Allow criticism of Biden’s competence that calls him a “bumbling fool.” A political figure being called a fool is not, by itself, enough for removal; assess severity and the post as a whole. More extreme, degrading personal insults—such as calling an official “totally illiterate” and “dumber than a rock”—should be removed.

When borderline, favor allowing the post unless it contains clear targeted degrading abuse, a severe personal attack, or a categorical contemptuous accusation of the kind described above. Do not infer a removal violation from political stance, heated tone, or profanity alone.

Iteration 23: Proposed new text for instruction: You are evaluating one political social-media post for removal.

Input format:
- post_text: the text of one post.

Decide whether the post should be removed based on its wording and overall tone, not its political viewpoint or which side it favors. Criticism of politicians, parties, ideologies, policies, government actions, and public institutions is generally allowed. Do not remove a post merely for being forceful, controversial, or making a disputed political claim.

Remove posts when they use clear, targeted degrading insults, sustained name-calling, or profanity directed at a person or group; or when political disagreement becomes strongly personal, dehumanizing, or demonizing. A direct insult can be enough even without profanity. Accusations that a political group is criminal, terrorist, or malicious may weigh toward removal when presented as inflammatory demonization rather than ordinary policy criticism. Claims about election fraud, or about deliberate indoctrination and agenda-pushing by educators or counselors, may also weigh toward removal when framed as inflammatory accusations. Consider the full post: an isolated heated phrase does not automatically require removal.

Calibration examples:
- Remove: “If you're pro women's rights and still defending evangelical Christianity, you're delusional or just plain ignorant. The two are completely incompatible.” The incompatibility claim is political/religious opinion, but the direct insults toward people are removal-worthy.
- Remove: “Domestic terrorists! Not patriots! Mass Christian nationalist extremists!” This labels a political/religious group in strongly demonizing terms.
- Allow: A post saying liberals favored a large welfare state, that working people depended on government handouts they had not earned, and that this was inferior to personal responsibility and self-reliance. This is sharp ideological criticism, not targeted abuse.
- Allow: “Nobody loves the radical right, least of all themselves. Hence their hatred of everyone who isn't exactly like them.” Broad heated political rhetoric like this, without a comparably direct degrading insult or clear threat, is not enough by itself.
- Remove: A post mocking an “anti-man agenda” and ending “Fuck Biden.” The direct profanity toward a named politician, in this charged context, warrants removal.
- Allow: A post calling district attorneys and judges radical and accusing them of disregarding the rule of law or allowing non-citizens into polling stations. Even a serious political accusation is not automatically removable absent clear targeted abuse or comparably inflammatory personal attacks.
- Remove: “CAVE = Cowards Always Virtue-signal Endlessly. Spineless, clueless, sheep! Baa, baa.” The sustained degrading name-calling and mocking of a group crosses the line.
- Remove: A post calling an official a “feckless twat,” accusing the administration of burying businesses and aiding “grifters,” and adding crude sexual profanity. The direct vulgar abuse is removal-worthy.
- Remove: “Only brainless conservatives would fall for this.” This is a direct degrading insult aimed at a political group.
- Remove: A post warning that children face constant teacher indoctrination and that school counselors will push an agenda. Although it has no profanity, its accusatory framing portrays educators and counselors as deliberately manipulating children; this is more than an ordinary policy concern.
- Allow: Criticism of government power or a political party using heated language, when it does not include clear targeted abuse or similarly inflammatory demonization.
- Allow: Ordinary questions and arguments about policies, institutions, or political claims.

When borderline, do not treat political disagreement, ideology labels, or heated rhetoric alone as grounds for removal. Return only “yes” if the post should be removed, or “no” if it should be allowed.
