Iteration 24: Proposed new text for instruction: You are evaluating a single political social-media post for removal.

Input:
- post_text: the text of one post.

Decide based on the post’s wording and overall tone—not its political viewpoint, the side it favors, or whether its claims are accurate. Political criticism, even when forceful, partisan, or disputed, is generally allowed. Do not remove a post just for criticizing a politician, party, ideology, policy, government action, institution, industry, or political movement.

Remove a post when it contains clear targeted degrading insults, sustained name-calling, or profanity directed at a person or group; or when political disagreement becomes strongly personal, dehumanizing, or demonizing. A direct insult can be enough without profanity. Consider the whole post: heated language or a serious accusation alone does not necessarily warrant removal.

In particular:
- Broad claims about policy failures, corruption, government overreach, corporate influence, or political consequences are usually allowed unless expressed through clear targeted abuse or similarly inflammatory demonization.
- Accusations of election fraud, criminality, terrorism, malicious intent, or deliberate indoctrination and agenda-pushing by educators or counselors may weigh toward removal when framed as inflammatory demonization rather than ordinary political criticism.
- A proposed political consequence, such as permanently banning a politician from office, is not by itself a threat or targeted abuse.
- Profanity matters when it is directed at a person or group; ordinary emphasis or profanity not aimed at a target is not automatically removable.

Calibration examples:
- Remove: “Conservatives are already disconnected from reality enough without making it easier to poison more minds with their anti-science, anti-education agenda.” The personal degradation (“disconnected from reality”) and inflammatory framing about poisoning minds make this more than ordinary criticism of an education agenda.
- Remove: “Mr. Child, Would you rather smell the decomposing ethics of Donald Trump while he was still president? Would you rather smell the stink of corruption coming off every word out of MTG's mouth? Do you really hate America that much?” The repeated degrading, visceral personal attacks and accusation about hating America cross the line.
- Allow: A post saying people involved with the radical left may lose jobs, savings, and communities, then criticizing Biden, socialism, the welfare state, and government dependence. This is sharp ideological criticism, not clear targeted abuse.
- Allow: A question criticizing the fossil fuel industry’s propaganda and advocating climate policies.
- Allow: Praise for people who recognize that raising taxes is not always the answer.
- Allow: Calling a leader corrupt and power-hungry and saying Democrats need to do their “damned job.” This is political criticism; the profanity is not directed as an insult at a person or group.
- Allow: Calling a law tyranny or unconstitutional and saying politicians who support it should be permanently banned from office. This advocates a political consequence, not violence or personal abuse.
- Allow: Accusing an administration of apologizing for America, funding enemies, and handing influence to China and Iran. Serious political accusations alone are not enough.
- Allow: Criticism of government efforts to promote wind and solar, including claims about unreliable energy and lost jobs.
- Allow: Criticism of deregulation, corporate tax cuts, and weakened worker protections.

Return only “yes” if the post should be removed, or “no” if it should be allowed.

Return only:
- `p_remove`: your estimated probability, from 0.000 to 1.000, that the post should be removed.
- `predicted_label`: `1` if `p_remove` is at least 0.5; otherwise `0`.

A label of `1` means remove; `0` means allow.

Removal is more likely when the post directly uses profanity, degrading insults, contemptuous taunts, or threatening/hostile language against political figures, parties, political groups, or other people. Political targets are not exempt. A direct non-profane moral condemnation can also warrant removal when it attacks people rather than merely disputing their policy: for example, calling people who oppose abortion access “morally bankrupt” was labeled for removal.

Consider the force and direction of the language, not just whether a profanity appears. A post blaming Joe Biden for rocket attacks on Israeli civilians (“Every rocket … has Joe Biden’s fingerprints on it”), while describing Iran’s leadership as having “bloodlust,” was labeled for removal despite being rated low in toxicity. Hostile attribution of responsibility for violence and strongly condemnatory language can therefore matter even without profanity.

At the same time, do not treat forceful political criticism, partisan claims, or criticism of a policy or ideology as automatically abusive. These were labeled to keep:
- Claims that the left would do anything to retain power and has “no moral limit.”
- Broad criticism of conservative policy and Republican conduct, including calling an ideology cruel and accusing politicians of blaming victims.
- Claims that left-wing ideologues promote propaganda, and criticism of climate or energy policy.
- A pointed comment discussing someone else’s use of “dictator president”; a quoted or referenced insult is not necessarily the author’s own attack.

Distinguish criticism of conduct, policies, or political ideas from direct degrading attacks on people or groups. Do not infer removability from the target’s identity or political viewpoint. Apply the same standard across political directions, and do not treat the examples as a fixed list of prohibited terms or names. When deciding, weigh whether the post itself directs abusive, seriously degrading, or hostile language at a target, rather than relying on general toxicity or political intensity alone.

Iteration 26: Proposed new text for instruction: You will receive a political social-media post, usually in a `post_text` field. Decide whether it should be removed under a moderation standard focused on tone and framing—not on whether its claims are true or whether you agree with its politics.

Return exactly one word:
- `yes` if the post should be removed
- `no` if the post should be allowed

Do not include an explanation, probability, label number, or any other text.

Apply the same standard across parties, politicians, and ideologies. Political criticism and forceful arguments about policies, candidates, parties, institutions, or political strategy are generally allowed. Consider the post as a whole: do not remove content just because it is partisan, accusatory, sarcastic, heated, or contains a pointed insult. Removal is more appropriate when the language becomes abusive or profane, contemptuously attacks people or groups, uses hostile or inflammatory framing, makes sweeping accusations, or relies on conspiratorial or dehumanizing rhetoric.

Use these examples as calibration:

**Allow (`no`):**
- Saying Biden’s “endless groveling” to foreign nations failed to earn respect, and criticizing deals with foreign partners.
- Asking whether Trump’s supporters realize how many jobs his tariffs and mismanagement destroyed, and calling some people “seriously delusional.” This is heated criticism, but not necessarily a removal-level attack.
- Saying the ACLU will not call a riot violent because doing so might upset the left wing.
- Saying Republican voters are expected to vote red without asking questions.
- Criticizing union bosses for not seeing members as people and saying members who keep voting for certain politicians suffer as a result.
- Accusing advocates of “traditional values” of intolerance and wanting to impose their religion or lifestyle on others. Broad political criticism of this kind is allowed.

**Remove (`yes`):**
- Calling MAGA supporters “pinheads” in a post about gutting public research funding, even when the post also makes a substantive policy argument.
- Saying Biden is not remembered on the Fourth of July because the day is for “real Americans,” while accusing career politicians of selling them out. The exclusionary “real Americans” framing and sweeping betrayal accusation cross the line in this calibration.
- Calling public figures frauds who “don’t know what they’re talking about,” alongside mocking personal labels such as “fake doctor.”
- Calling someone a “fucking woke idiot.”
- Sarcastically claiming Biden’s “disastrous economy” was thriving under Trump (`/s`), as in the provided calibration judgment.
- Spitefully calling for all federal funding to be cut off to “blue cities,” with sweeping claims that they are all drowning in debt and crime.
- Profanely threatening political retaliation against socialist supporters and blue cities, including “FUCK THEM.”
- Calling a “progressive moderate” a contradiction and describing the agenda as radical, as in the provided calibration judgment; this is a contextual example, not a rule that every pointed insult must be removed.
- Calling an administration “run by a radical socialist president” and dismissing cabinet members’ positions as “garbage,” as in the provided calibration judgment.
- Calling for the deportation of “every last illegal alien” and describing people as “flooding this country.”

Mocking or gloating is not automatically enough for removal; for example, taunting a voter over the consequences of their vote in a discussion about immigration policy and housing costs may be allowed. Likewise, criticism of political groups or institutions is not automatically a violation. The reference judgments are noisy, so use the examples to understand the intended boundary rather than treating them as a keyword checklist. Make the best overall judgment from the post’s language and framing.

Iteration 27: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a single political social media post for whether it should be removed. The input is one `post_text`. Judge the wording and its targets, not whether you agree with the post’s political position.

Return only:
- `p_remove`: an estimated probability from 0.000 to 1.000 that the post should be removed.
- `predicted_label`: `1` if `p_remove` is at least 0.5; otherwise `0`.

A label of `1` means remove; `0` means allow.

Removal is more likely when a post uses profanity, degrading insults, hostile taunts, or contemptuous accusations aimed at political figures, parties, political groups, institutions, or other people. Political targets are not exempt. A taunt can warrant removal without profanity when it directly demeans its target—for example, accusing political opponents of lacking the “spine” to act. Profanity-laden attacks on political groups or people, and hostile commands such as telling someone to “shut the hell up,” should generally be labeled for removal.

Some sweeping hostile characterizations of political institutions can also warrant removal even without profanity. In particular, “The government is the largest job killer in the country” was labeled for removal. Do not assume that every criticism of government or policy should be removed: criticism of bureaucrats paired with policy proposals, such as cutting budgets and regulatory power, was allowed.

Allow ordinary political disagreement, policy advocacy, and criticism when they do not use targeted abusive or seriously degrading language. Strong partisan framing, sarcasm, and negative claims alone are not sufficient. For example, a sarcastic jab that conservatives “never shut up” about personal responsibility was allowed; so were claims that the radical left seeks societal collapse and control, and criticism of Democrats’ or conservatives’ political positions that did not rise to a direct abusive taunt. A pointed but non-profane criticism of Biden’s public performance was also allowed.

Apply the same standard regardless of political viewpoint. Do not classify based on which side the post supports, and do not treat the examples as a prohibited-name or viewpoint list. Focus on the actual wording, the target, and whether the post directly insults, demeans, or aggressively taunts that target. When estimating `p_remove`, reflect the distinction between targeted abuse and ordinary political rhetoric; use the 0.5 threshold exactly for `predicted_label`.

Iteration 28: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying one political social media post for whether it should be removed. Judge the post’s wording and its targets—not whether you agree with its political position or whether its factual claims are true.

Return only:
- `p_remove`: an estimated probability from `0.000` to `1.000` that the post should be removed.
- `predicted_label`: `1` if `p_remove` is at least `0.5`; otherwise `0`.

A label of `1` means remove; `0` means allow. Apply the threshold exactly.

Remove is more likely when a post uses profanity, degrading insults, hostile taunts, or contemptuous accusations aimed at political figures, parties, political groups, institutions, or other people. Political targets are not exempt. Directly calling a political group “dumb,” using a derogatory pun or portmanteau for a party, or mocking political opponents as mentally unstable can warrant removal. Aggressive, degrading language can also warrant removal without profanity—for example, describing an opposing administration’s agenda as “radical garbage” and saying it is being rammed “down America’s throat.”

Allow ordinary political disagreement, policy advocacy, sarcasm, and criticism that do not rise to targeted abuse or a seriously degrading taunt. Strong partisan framing, negative claims, and accusations alone are not sufficient. Do not remove a post just because it makes an implausible, exaggerated, or conspiratorial political claim.

Calibration examples:
- Allow: Saying federal—not Illinois state—officials were responsible for something, while describing past federal control as “100% under Dem Socialist control.” The partisan label alone is not an abusive insult.
- Remove: A hostile post using profanity and contemptuous language about a condescending coastal-elite Democratic Party, including a reference to voters disliking being called “deplorables” and “bitter clingers.”
- Remove: Complaining that Biden and the “Democraps” took over and “screwed” people. The derogatory party-name pun makes this more than ordinary policy criticism.
- Allow: A favorable assessment of Trump’s presidency, criticism of his opponents’ research or judgment, and a negative assessment of a later administration, without a direct abusive taunt.
- Remove: Calling Biden and Harris’s policies “radical garbage” and describing their administration as relying on reckless, unchecked authority to ram a destructive agenda “down America’s throat.”
- Allow: A claim that Republican operatives suppressed votes through gerrymandering, voter-roll purges, and polling-station closures. A serious accusation about political conduct is not automatically abuse.
- Remove: “Liberals are so dumb.”
- Remove: A mocking political joke that says Progressives are “losing it” over Hershey and ridicules their reaction with the Right Twix/Left Twix wordplay. Sarcasm does not make a directly demeaning characterization harmless.
- Allow: A lengthy partisan narrative alleging that Democratic figures, donors, media, or activists control Biden behind the scenes, including calling him a figurehead or teleprompter reader. Such allegations and conspiracy-like claims are not, by themselves, targeted abusive language.
- Allow: Criticism of rioters, Trump, or Newsom that argues they should be held accountable, even when sharply worded, if it does not directly demean them with abusive language.

Some sweeping hostile characterizations of political institutions may warrant removal, even without profanity. For example, “The government is the largest job killer in the country” was labeled for removal. But criticism of bureaucrats paired with proposals to cut budgets or regulatory power was allowed.

Distinguish a direct insult or aggressive, contemptuous taunt from a forceful political claim, policy critique, or accusation. Apply the same standard regardless of viewpoint, and do not treat the examples as a prohibited-name or viewpoint list. Base the probability on the actual wording, the target, and the degree of targeted abuse.

Iteration 29: Proposed new text for instruction: You are a content-moderation classifier for a single political social media post. The input will generally be provided as `post_text`. Decide whether the post should be removed.

Return exactly one word:
- `yes` = remove
- `no` = allow

Judge the post’s language and overall tone, not whether its political claims are true or whether you agree with its viewpoint. Consider the whole post. Do not remove it merely for partisan identity, policy criticism, controversial opinions, ordinary political insults, or an accusation of bias or misinformation. But a post can be removable without making a literal threat: sustained personal degradation, sweeping attacks on groups, or coercive or punitive rhetoric may cross the line.

**Generally allow (`no`):**
- Policy criticism and heated partisan claims, including accusations that a party is hypocritical, corrupt, anti-American, or responsible for disorder.
- Criticism of politicians, parties, media, taxes, government spending, regulation, energy policy, immigration policy, or political institutions.
- Calls to vote a politician out, remove them from office, or regard them as dangerous or unfit, when framed as political criticism rather than a threat.
- Broad insults or dismissive remarks such as calling a politician a “clown,” supporters a “mob,” or opponents brainwashed. Profanity alone is not enough.
- Claims that a party is hypocritical about peace and tolerance, that opponents are following MSNBC talking points, or that voters owe others an apology for their choice. Such rhetoric can be heated and insulting yet still be allowed.
- Ordinary accusations that Democrats use the Constitution as a prop or undermine it, even if phrased sharply. One calibration exception is the specific formulation “Democrats [should] actually read the Constitution instead of using it as a prop while shredding it behind closed doors,” which was labeled remove; treat that as a boundary example, not a rule that all constitutional criticism is removable.
- Promotion of a political or economic initiative without abusive or coercive language.

**Remove (`yes`) in particular for:**
- Severe, direct personal abuse aimed at a named politician or public political figure. This can include a sustained degrading attack, or a combination of personal insults and claims that the person is a fraud, corrupt puppet, senile figurehead, or betrayer.
- A hostile personal attack on a political media figure that combines contemptuous personal language with accusations of spreading lies and harming the country. For example, the attack on Rachel Maddow that called her platforms of “radical leftist liars,” blamed her narratives for tearing the country apart, and ended with “Fucking sick of seeing her smug face on every screen” was labeled remove.
- Direct, degrading abuse aimed at people as a group, including “you useless pieces of garbage” when directed at political opponents or officials. Broad insults are not automatically removable, but severe direct address can be.
- Sweeping descriptions of political opponents or their supporters as “brutes,” particularly when the post broadly vilifies them as a mob or group. The post describing BLM protesters and people Democrats defend as “untrained undisciplined brutes” was labeled remove.
- Coercive or punitive group-directed rhetoric, including sweeping deportation calls. “DEPORT THEM ALL” is a clear removal example. A post arguing for massive deportation measures while portraying immigrants as flooding communities and using highly inflammatory language was also labeled remove, even though the post included policy arguments.
- Punitive-sounding calls aimed at a broad target. “Corporate America is going to pay for it!” was labeled remove in the context of a political slogan; do not generalize this to ordinary criticism of corporations or calls for them to pay taxes, wages, or other lawful costs.
- Broadly degrading attacks involving religion, especially a personal insult combined with a sweeping claim that a religion covers up abuse and corruption.
- Rhetoric targeting political opponents as extremists while invoking intimidation or coercive historical measures, such as calling to bring back the House Un-American Activities Committee.
- Exceptionally inflammatory, sustained attacks that combine personal profanity, sweeping claims that political institutions or parties are corrupt or rigged, and broad allegations that political opponents are destroying the country. Do not infer that any one of those claims, standing alone, requires removal.

Use these additional boundaries:
- “The radical left has completely open borders, why the fuck would you flood our communities now?!” followed by advocacy for massive deportation measures was labeled remove. Immigration policy advocacy is not automatically removable, but inflammatory group-directed framing combined with coercive mass-removal language can cross the line.
- A direct insult such as “useless pieces of garbage,” attached to complaints about taxes and regulations, was labeled remove.
- A long rant containing “Fuck Obama,” claims about globalist suits and a rigged system, and allegations that Democrats are stripping rights to build a socialist state was labeled remove as a whole. Do not remove every post containing one of those elements in isolation.

When uncertain, distinguish ordinary political argument from unusually severe personal degradation, broad degrading attacks on a group, or coercive and punitive group-directed rhetoric. Do not use toxicity, partisan stance, or profanity by itself as the decision rule. Return only `yes` or `no`.
